# Rules of Conduct for Public Comment at Council Meetings $ ^{*} $

Members of the public may speak at a Council meeting upon recognition by the Mayor. Public comment may only occur during periods designated on the agenda for public comment or a public hearing. A person may speak for up to two (2) minutes during each of the two public comment periods on agenda items or non-agenda items. Waivers of the time requirement may only be granted in the discretion of the Mayor, and waivers to speak at a time other than a designated comment period may only be granted by the Council. Any person wishing to make a presentation longer than two minutes or requiring audio-visual equipment is asked to contact the City Clerk requesting to appear on a future agenda.

A person may only address Council from the podium. Only one person may occupy the podium at a time. All remarks are to be directed to the Mayor and Council. Speakers are not to engage in direct dialog with other meeting attendees.

Any person who violates the Rules of Conduct, disturbs the peace at the meeting, and/or interferes with the meeting may be warned, ordered to be seated, removed, and/or ticketed.

 $ ^{*} $This summarizes Council Resolution 04-18. Complete Rules, including guidelines for considering waivers, are available in the Council Chambers and from the City Clerk.

## PROCLAMATION

Mayor Kennedy read the attached proclamation for Mark Savedes of Alekos.

Mr. Savedes of Alekos thanked everyone and stated it has been an honor, and 30 years ago on his mom's birthday, it is hard to believe Alekos is still going strong and making restaurant food and catering. He then stated it has been great to be part of this community for so long, and 3 decades is a crazy long time, but he wouldn't have it any other way, they love South Lyon.

## PUBLIC COMMENT

Ralph Dunigan of 216 E Lake stated he doesn't understand the consent agreement. He stated when the cruise first started, they were asked and they said they would come every year to ask if they wanted it. They said they would try it the first year, the following year when they came, they said they didn't want this, and they said it is too late the city has already approved it. It's been approved each year since, without asking them. The Police have a form on their website for block parties, and every person on the block has to sign it or it isn't allowed. That is basically a block party because you're closing the street, and without asking them. He again stated that for a block party, everyone has to agree before the road can be closed, but the city is basically having a block party 5-6 times a year without asking us and assuming that they are ok with it. He further stated they don't want it, there have been numerous problems, and cars have been hit and there is no place to park. He wakes up at 3 in the morning and he isn't allowed to park in his driveway until 9:30 at night. He said he can't park at 9:30 at night and get up at 3:00 in the morning and drive a truck all day. He then stated his wife gets home at 6:00 and in her high heels, purse and brief case, she has to park a block away and walk home. He doesn't think anyone else would do that, and he is very upset that it was approved, and no one asked them. He stated he has spoken with 3 or 4 councilmembers telling them they didn't want this last year.

Stacy Kissel of 228 E Lake Street a few houses down from the previous speaker and she is here to support her neighbor. She stated they were also asked one time at the beginning and they were supportive of it, but over the years, it has grown cumbersome. It is very large, and it is one thing to live close to town, but it is another thing to have strangers camping out in chairs on your front lawn. She stated she is also concerned that now that Lake Street Tavern is closed, which was their headquarters, she doesn't know how that will spill out into the town. She further stated that is 2 of them on a block with 4 houses that aren't in support of the events. She isn't asking to not hold the events, but look at where it is mapped out. The way it is currently set up, it is very loud, they can't have their windows open, there is a DJ playing the same music every time just blaring. She stated it is an annoyance more than anything. She further stated she isn't asking for it to be shut down, she wants everyone to have a good time, but the homeowners would like to be considered.

Jennifer Dunigan of 216 E Lake Street stated she wanted to make sure everyone received the letter she sent asking Council to vote no on the event. She stated it is a page and half letter stating why they are asking for Council to not shut down Motorfest, or car cruise, but to vote no on the closing of the street. She further stated she is sorry if they didn't read the letter, but she would hope they would listen to their constituents.

## FIRE CHIEF REPORT

Fire Marshal Moynihan stated as of today, they have responded to 356 instances. He stated in the month of April, they have done 14 business inspections, 7 plan reviews, and they have started hydro flushing. He said they are currently doing the northwest quadrant of the city. He then reminded everyone of the open house on May 18th from 11am to 3pm. Councilmember Bogert asked if they did Lexington Place. Mr. Moynihan stated they have only done Trotters Point as of now. Councilmember Bogert said he has heard some complaints about brown water. Mr. Moynihan stated they received a call from the water department asking about the different areas they have done. Mayor Kennedy stated he has heard some complaints about yellow water from Trotter's residents so this makes sense. Mr. Moynihan stated they will be putting the areas that they will be flushing on their social media.

## POLICE CHIEF REPORT

Chief Baaki stated he was happy to help with the Creek Clean Up yesterday, there were a lot of people there. He and the Fire Chief were working behind Kroger. He then stated he has been working on their frequencies with Livingston County dispatch so they will be able to communicate with Green Oak Township and Livingston County. Councilmember Bogert asked Chief Baaki to speak about the speed limit signs. Chief Baaki stated they have a two total, and one is near the railroad tracks on Pontiac Trail. He said the batteries last about 2 weeks then they have to bring them in and charge the batteries. He then explained the reason they use them. He said they count the number of cars that pass, as well as the speed. He stated it will give you the average speed when you gather the data. He further stated it was on Hagadorn for a couple of weeks, and it counted 5,000 cars, with an average speed of 19 mph. It is valuable information which can show if the speed limit on a road needs to be changed. Councilmember Bogert asked about the water wars that the Police posted on social media. Chief Baaki stated it is harmless, seniors go out and try to spray each other with water guns. Usually it is harmless, we don't get many complaints about it. It's all in good fun, and they don't try to harm each other. Councilmember Kivell asked if a solar panel could be added. Chief Baaki stated they do have one, but it is much easier to maneuver it around to different areas. Mayor Kennedy stated he is aware that Chief Baaki met with the City Manager, and the organizers of the cruise in shows and looked at some things to help avoid some of the things in the past happening. Chief Baaki stated they will have officers in the area, and we'll make sure people aren't in people's yards, or blocking the sidewalks, and he will be there as well. Mayor Kennedy stated another item discussed was reserving the parking spots for the people that live in that area and providing a placard to easily identify who is able to park in that area. He further stated it sounds like about 11 spots and they will be monitored. Chief Baaki we will be monitoring and out and about.

## NEW BUSINESS

### 1. Award bid of Eagle Pointe/Brookfield paving contract

City Manager Zelenak stated to make further improvements to roads within the City of South Lyon and as the second set of projects associated with our Road Bond project, we have solicited bids for the Eagle Pointe/Brookfield Subdivision paving project. We have received 3 bids, which range from $2,078,944.43 to a high of $2,454,038. The lowest responsible bidder was from Florence Cement. We are waiting for their project schedules to see if the project will come out of the 2023/2024 and/or 2024/2025 budget years. Mayor Kennedy stated he knows we are waiting for their schedule, but he asked if they will be working on both projects at the same time, or separately. Mr. Darga from HRC stated he doesn't know, but they are supposed to be completed by September. City Manager Zelenak stated he will be updating Council and also have meetings with the residents of the subdivisions for more information.

2. First reading of amendment of an ordinance pertaining to the city council meeting time

At recent City Council meetings, discussion was held in regards to possibly changing the meeting time for City Council meetings, from 7:30 to 6:30. The proposed ordinance amendment amends chapter 2, article II, Section 1-31 and will change the meeting time beginning July 1, 2024.

### 3. Cost participant agreement- Pontiac Trail project

City Manager Zelenak stated the City of South Lyon is partnering with Oakland County and the Road Commission for Oakland County in a road paving and storm sewer project. The total cost for the project is approximately $8 millions dollars. The first part of this project is to complete preliminary engineering and design at a total cost of $513,446. Our portion will be paid for using our Tri Party Program funding in the amount of $230,758 which shall be paid through equal contributions by the Road Commission, Oakland County and the City of South Lyon. ($76,929) approving of this cost participation agreement

<a id="p7"></a>
*[Page 7 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=7)* (read by OCR; check the original)

will authorize the payment and the process for the preliminary design and engineering on the project to proceed. The funding for the project will be paid out of account #401-451-802-130. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated she understands the initial review, but the total cost is $8 million. Is that a tri party share? City Manager Zelenak stated it is not. The tri party agreement is for dollars allocated between Oakland County and the Road Commission and the city for certain road projects we have in the city. He stated we have approximately $250,000 total allocated within the tri party funds that we have saved up for several years. He further stated a third of that is for us to pay for. The majority is being paid for through federal grants that we received close to $5 million dollars. The road commission is the lead agency on this and they will be handling the project. We will be paying for our part as well as any sanitary sewer or water main replacements that may be needed. Also, any streetscapes improvements that may need to be made. He further stated this is early in the project and the scope of the project is still being determined based upon the preliminary design to be done by HRC who is also working with the Road Commission on this job. As we get closer to developing the project, we will have public meetings to let everyone know the scope of the project. Once we know the depth of the sewer system, we will know the cost participation we will need. Councilmember Kurtzweil asked what the time table is for the cost is going to be because the cost could go up over the next year or two.

Motion by Kurtzweil, supported by Mosier

## MANAGER'S REPORT

City Manager Zelenak stated the Andover Bridge was installed last Friday and will be open for public use very soon. He then stated he wanted to let the public know about additional road projects that will be done later this year. In addition to what was discussed tonight. They will be repaving from the rail road tracks to Silver Lake Road and paving 11 Mile Road from Pontiac Trail to Milford Road and they will be paving Martindale Road from 10 Mile to 11 Mile Road. He expects to get a time frame somewhere around mid-June. He stated he will update everyone in regards to when they start and detours if any. It is a mill and overlay project, so he expects the roads to remain open. Councilmember Hansen asked if they are going to stagger road projects. City Manager Zelenak stated he doesn't think they will be working on them at the same time, but they will be trying to get them completed as soon as possible after school is out. They are being paid for by the Oakland County Road Commission, not our road bonds.

Councilmember Kurtzweil thanked the Dunigan's for coming in and speaking, and it is always important to listen to our community. She apologized because she was in court today and didn't have access to her cell phone, but she did receive the email and she will get back to them on that. She then asked if the Dunigan's were invited to the meetings with the Chief when the plan was made. City Manager Zelenak stated he doesn't believe so. Councilmember Kurtzweil then stated maybe they can meet with the City Manager and Chief Baaki to go over some of their concerns, which one is that no one asked them ahead of time. It is important to listen to what our constituents say. They shouldn't have to come to a meeting, they should have been called or asked. Maybe a meeting to go over some of the concerns and make sure they were addressed. City Manager Zelenak stated he knows the organizers are going to be in contact with them, but he doesn't know when. He further stated Council votes to approve or not approve the events, but he will make sure the meeting happens and to have their concerns addressed. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated she had sent the City Manager some materials in regards to the West Lake property. She then stated she wanted to bring to Council her research. She stated with the issue with the project and the wetlands, she is a little concerned and surprised that the solution to the road in the wetland didn't provide an opportunity for some alternate road materials. She said there are alternate road materials that can be used when constructing a road through wetlands. The advantage to alternate road materials has the advantage of not being as restrictive of the water flow that happens when you put a road through wetlands. She further stated there are alternatives out there, the state of South Carolina, particularly in Durham, they are doing incredible work in moving in areas with construction in wetlands. They are putting in roads, and resources accesses that are made with materials that are less impactful on the wetlands. She further stated she would maybe like to see some of those options that the developer could provide. She gets the development needs to be done and that we can't hold it up, but on the other hand, there are other options out there that she didn't hear discussed and one of them would be to look at alternative road components. She stated she spent hours looking at construction of the road itself and looking at the aggregate materials. She further stated just by enlarging the aggregate materials that are used as the base of the road would allow for more water flow through that road, rather than putting in a highly compacted material. She further stated that is one of the solutions. She stated maybe we look at more environmentally sensitive road construction options so we can mitigate the impact on the wetlands. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated she spent time looking at what Texas is doing with constructing wetlands. She then stated she won't go into why the wetlands are important and the ability of the wetlands to purify the water. However, Texas is in need of a purification system and they are moving toward the wetland concept. So, they are constructing wetlands. So, she is saying to herself, if she was a creative developer, why is she not presenting to Council that this will impact 3,000 feet of wetlands, but she would construct 3,000 feet of wetlands to replace what she would be impacting by the road. That is another option that is out there. She would consider that and we need some good solutions for serious issues. Councilmember Kurtzweil asked if they were to put up the Roper barn today, would they have to go before the Planning Commission. City Manager Zelenak stated it would need to come before the building department and city council, but from his understanding they are far from being ready to do so. We have not received any plans; we just know they have components of a barn in a location being stored. The city has allocated funds for the foundation and paving and that will have to be worked out as well. Councilmember Kurtzweil then asked if they would have to pay for permits or would the city waive those fees. City Manager Zelenak stated it is a city project we would basically be paying ourselves. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated she read the press release from EGLE and they have a grant program for water clean ups, and she asked if the city has ever applied. City Manager Zelenak stated he and Councilmember Hansen discussed that this year and we need to know all the funds that were allocated as part of the project that we could use to supply them with that information to apply for the grant. If we are getting funded for those by donations, or volunteer hours, we can't necessarily collect for those. We

## COUNCIL COMMENTS

Councilmember Hansen stated the weather started out a bit chilly, but the volunteers still showed up in record numbers for the 20th Annual Clean Up and Earth Day event. He stated more than 130 volunteers showed up to the event, including city staff as well as support staff. He further stated the State's Lieutenant Governor and the State's EGLE Director were in attendance to mark the occasion. He then stated Lieutenant Governor Gilchrist spoke to the influence of environmental events like the South Lyon Creek Clean Up and he thanked the volunteers for continuing the cleanup efforts. EGLE Director Roos talked about some of the efforts his office was working on to clean up the environment state wide and thanked the volunteers for doing their part. He stated the volunteers put in a lot of effort and completely filled the dumpster, even the Lieutenant Governor helped to clean up the Yerkes Drain. He then thanked all the volunteers that helped to make it such a successful event. He then thanked all the sponsors, South Lyon Boy Scout Troop 228, Cub Scouts Pack 38, which were the originators of the event, and the South Lyon DPW, the storm and waste water management planning program, the South Lyon Water Department, the Wellhead Protection Program, Michigan Seamless Tube, Vibe Credit Union, Martin's Hardware, Tormina's Pizza, Kensington Valley Civilian Club, Larry and Dylan Ledbetter, and Witches Hat Brewing Company. He then stated this was a huge milestone for the city, 20 years of clean ups. He further stated the first clean up 20 years ago had only 4 volunteers, and we had over 130 volunteers and the Lieutenant Governor. He then stated 20 years down, and this community has the drive to keep going as long as it's needed. He wished everyone a Happy Earth Day. He also stated they received a certificate of recognition from Alyssa Slotkins' office for the milestone of the event as well as thanking the volunteers for putting forth the event for as long as we have and for continuing the event.

Councilmember Kurtzweil congratulated Mark Savedes and his wife, Mom and brother and sister-in-law. She stated he has a great restaurant and well loved in the community. She further stated everyone knows what he does and he is one of those business owners that doesn't ask for recognition, and he does a lot of charity in this town and never asks for a pat on the back. She then thanked him for his incredible humility and dedication to the City of South Lyon and he is an incredible individual. Councilmember Kurtzweil thanked everyone that showed up for the Creek Clean Up, and especially those that helped her out of the ditch. She stated it was a great event and they pulled a lot of garbage out. She also thanked everyone that attended the Roper Barn Fundraiser at the VFW. It was a great time and great turnout. She then reminded everyone the Cultural Arts Commission is celebrating the National Poets month and has its display of poet boards throughout the city, and there is a special dedication to Dayna Johnston by one of our poets and residents, Josie Kearns. She stated it is located next to South Lyon Village Bakery and it's the one with a bow on top. She stated everyone should walk around town and enjoy poetry this month. She then thanked DPW for installing all of the poetry boards. She stated the Cultural Arts Commission is having its A&E event, which is Arts and Education show later this week with opening night being on May 3rd. She further stated this show is a great exhibit of the young talent in our high schools, and she

He stated a few of us went to the rededication event and reception and it was great to see all the great work and great people that are involved in this group. He stated this is the 3 $ ^{rd} $ year hosting the market and it's a great opportunity for young people to learn how to run a business, and how to create products, set prices and how to market. He further stated he has seen a lot of great things happen through that. It will be at South Lyon Biggby.

Councilmember Mosier stated we have two weeks to the opening market, and with 71 vendors, there are a lot of things to look at, lots of products to choose and a lot of food.

Councilmember Kivelli stated he was talking with Director Roos and the West Lake Development came up and he walked him through the complications and some people thinking the bridge is too much, and he talked to him about the danger of driving in an area that would have that much car flow that's not well suited for it, and its dangerous and they were hoping to get a yes vote so they would end up having a look at it, then the decision would be a lot easier whether there is a down side to having the bridge in there. He further stated they wanted the experts and he was dying to get a shot at it. He stated he hopes at some point Council will entertain that and see if it could come into fruition. He then stated he voted no on the resolution changing the time of the council meetings because he has a concern with people that work late and they don't have an opportunity to come speak to Council directly, rather than having to email. He then stated he is happy we have a full board on Parks and Recreation. They will be very active this year, and a lot of decisions to make.

Mayor Kennedy shared something that took place recently. He stated he recently had the pleasure of addressing the second graders at four of the elementary schools in our area as part of their Local Government module in school. It was obvious that the teachers had worked with their classes to identify some thoughtful and meaningful questions regarding the city and the mayor's responsibilities. However, once those were exhausted, the questions changed and he had the opportunity to explain that he doesn't live in a mansion, don't have a secret service detail, he does not get to fly in a helicopter, does not drive a Lamborghini and have not met the President. He then stated they asked, "How old are you?" He stated he was up for the question and replied well, how old do you think I am? The first reply was 39, to which he was good with that; the next was 45, and he was good with that answer as well. Then he stated he told them how old he was and the response from one end of the cafeteria to the other was universal and overwhelming... "WOAH!!!" One student punctuated the moment even further by exclaiming that he was as old as his grandpa. He then stated after the decibel level dropped, he said so, you see, you are never too old or too young to help out in your community and he would encourage you all to remember that and share that with your friends and family and find ways you can help as well. He further stated he encourages everyone to see what they can do to help, when the call goes out for volunteers to serve on boards or commissions like Jordan and Melissa have done by joining the Parks and Recreation Commission, or to help with Pumpkinfest, Christmas in July, South Lyon Live and South Lyon Eve. Remember, you're never too young, or too old, to pitch in and lend a hand in the community.

Respectfully submitted.

Mayor Steve Kennedy

## Office of the Mayor

## PROCLAMATION

WHEREAS, Aleko's Restaurant and Catering has been serving delicious food in South Lyon for over 30 years, owner Mark Savedes describes Aleko's as a service-oriented business; and

WHEREAS, Aleko's Restaurant and Catering has been rated one of the best restaurants in the area and has been meeting the needs of food lovers in the South Lyon area; and

WHEREAS, Aleko's Restaurant and Catering has been an important, caring part of the community contributing to many causes and needs without hesitation, volunteering for local and international projects, and surprising local residents with Christmas gifts or just paying bills; and

WHEREAS, the personal motto of Mark Savedes is “To whom much is given, much is expected” and prefers to live for a greater purpose; and

NOW THEREFORE, I, Stephen B. Kennedy, Mayor of the City of South Lyon on behalf of the City Council and the entire community offer congratulations to Aleko's Restaurant and Catering as they celebrate over 30 years in business and wish them many more years of success.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Stephen B. Kennedy, Mayor

# AGENDA NOTE Consent Agenda

MEETING DATE: May 13, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Mayor Kennedy

AGENDA TOPIC: Appointment for Cultural Arts Commission

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The Cultural Arts Commission consists of 5 members, and currently have 1 seat available. Members appointed by the Mayor and approved by Council. Each member will serve a 3-year term.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Application from Joseph Matuzak dated April 18, 2024 to The Cultural Arts Commission

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve or not approve the nomination by the Mayor of Joseph Matuzak to the Cultural Arts Commission

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by _____, supported by _____ to approve Mayor Kennedy's nomination of Joseph Matuzik to the Cultural Arts Commission

# CANDIDATE QUESTIONNAIRE

The City of South Lyon wishes to thank you for your interest in serving as a Volunteer Citizen Representative on a Board, Commission or Committee. Your Candidate Questionnaire will be kept on file and entered for consideration for posted openings on any Board, Commission or Committee that you expressed an interest in for a period of two years. Please feel free to submit an updated Candidate Questionnaire to the City Clerk at any time.

Please be advised that the information contained in this Questionnaire is not confidential, and will be reviewed by the Mayor, City Council and other appropriate personnel as vacancies or openings occur on the various Boards, Commissions and Committees. This Candidate Questionnaire may also be included in any City Council Meeting Packet which is published and made available for public inspection in print and on the Internet. Your address, phone numbers and email will not be published, even in the Meeting Packet.

Please be advised that even though you may submit an application to serve on a Board, Commission or Committee, it does not guarantee that you will be appointed to that Board, Commission or Committee.

BOARDS/COMMISSIONS/COMMITTEES ON WHICH YOU WANT TO SERVE (please check up to five applicable boxes; see attachment for descriptions):

# CITY OF SOUTH LYON – BOARDS AND COMMISSIONS

City Council: Elected Office (7 Members) – Meets second and fourth Monday of the month at 7:30 pm in the Auditorium. Nominating petitions required. Petitions are available at the City Clerk's office.

Planning Commission: Appointed Office (7 Members) – Meets the second and fourth Thursdays of the month at 7:00 pm in the Auditorium. The Planning Commission prepares a Master Plan for existing and future land uses within the City; develops a Zoning Ordinance to implement the Master Plan, reviews change of land use requests, site plans and related approvals for development, and/or change of occupancy; and assists with the development of a Capital Improvement Plan.

Zoning Board of Appeals: Appointed Office (5 Members) – Meets the third Thursday of the month at 7:00 pm in the Auditorium. The ZBA is a quasi-judicial board that considers appeals, variance requests, and ordinance interpretation. Plus two (2) alternate positions, as needed.

Cable Commission: Appointed Office (3 Members) – Meets the second Wednesday of the month at 7:00 pm in the North Conference Room. The Cable Commission is responsible for the content on the City's local government Channel 19. They record football games, interviews with local business owners, and informational content for the City.

Cultural Arts Commission: Appointed Office (6 Members) – Meets the first Thursday and third Wednesday of the month at 6:00 in the South Conference Room. The Cultural Arts Commission encourages, facilitates and promotes Culture and Art in the City, such as promotional and educational activities to increase public awareness of the necessity and desirability of visual arts, music and culture in the community.

Parks and Recreation Commission: Appointed Office (7 Members) – Meets the second Wednesday of the month at 7:00 pm in the South Conference Room. This Commission works with the City to develop a 5-year Parks and Recreation Master Plan. The Commission ensures that the Parks and Recreation Master Plan is being followed and will make recommendations for other improvements to recreation facilities.

Downtown Development Authority: Appointed Office (9 Members) – Meets the second Thursday of the month at 8:00 am in the South Conference Room. This Board develops a Downtown Development Plan and oversees the budget for tax increment financing with the DDA District.

Ethics Commission: Appointed Office (5 Members) – Will meet on an as needed basis to ensure that the City's Ethic Ordinance is being followed.

## Senior Survey Specialist, University of Michigan Institute for Social Research, 2016-2018

- Project Manager for numerous initiatives, development and stewardship of survey design, including trouble shooting design flaws and redesign. Manage budgets for multiple projects with individual budgets over $1 million.

- Provide support for numerous surveys within and outside the Survey Lab.

• Supervision of numerous data collectors, coders, administrative staff.

- Continuing as Project Manager for Survey of Consumer Attitudes (SCA). Responsible for planning and coordination of data collection, training and direction of Production Managers, coordination of goals and budget with Principal Investigator and study staff.

- Project or Production Manager for Detroit Metropolitan Area Community Study, CogUSA Saliva study, Panel Study of Income Dynamics Well-Being study, Life History Mail Study, others.

## Survey Specialist Intermediate, University of Michigan Institute for Social Research 2007-2016

- Project Manager for Survey of Consumer Attitudes (SCA) since September 2006. Responsible for planning and coordination of data collection, interviewer recruitment, and training, coordination of goals and budget with Principal Investigator.

- Project or Production Manager for Social Relations Wave 3, Army STARRS Pre-Post Deployment Study, Time 3, Promoting Well-Being Across Adulthood, Detroit Area Study of Social Relations and Well-Being, and Description of Reading Instruction studies, among others.

## Survey Specialist Associate, University of Michigan Institute for Social Research 2006-2007

- Project Manager for Survey of Consumer Attitudes (SCA) Mixed mode initiative, which explored developing a CATI-Web mixed mode instrument.

- Provided training and support for new SCA Team Leaders, troubleshooting and assistance with experiment implementation and reporting.

Also generated respondent contact materials and updated project database both by documenting respondent contact and by incorporating online progress reports to reflect ongoing project activities.

- Conducted telephone interviews for the Attitudes on Terrorism Preparedness study and the University of Michigan Surveys of Consumer Attitudes (SCA).

- Worked on tracking and data verification for the Residential Energy Consumption study and Monitoring the Future.

## Nonprofit Technology Consultant and Writer 2000-2004

With focus on the arts, clients included Greater Flint Arts Council, ArtServe Michigan, the Michigan Association of Community Arts Agencies, the Mid-America Arts Alliance, the Triangle Arts Trust, the Ford Foundation, Illinois Arts Council, Americans for the Arts, Arts Midwest, Imagining America (University of Michigan), Flint Cultural Center, National Endowment for the Arts, others.

Training, technological assessment, mission analysis and discussion, database and web design, Cold Fusion scripting, articles, etc. Projects include both on-site training and consulting (in Kenya, Trinidad, India, and various places in the U.S) as well as electronic projects.

## Director, Arts Wire, a program of the New York Foundation for the Arts 1995-2000

Responsible for implementing the Arts Wire program, including aspects of budgeting and financial management, fundraising, grant writing, personnel management and procurement, setting program goals and objectives, and creating scenarios for achieving those goals.

Managed a virtual office that included staff members scattered throughout the country, and acted as primary spokesperson for the organization, including a large number of public speaking engagements.

Guided training processes, looked to develop new methods and workshops and presented lessons learned from the same.

### Executive Director of Buckham Fine Arts Project, Flint, MI. 1990-92

- Responsible for all operational, financial, and programming activities of artist-run multidisciplinary arts organization in consultation with Board of Directors.

## OTHER ACTIVITIES

Grant review panels – National Endowment for the Arts, Greater Flint Arts Council, Regional Arts Partnerships, Progressive Technology Project, Michigan Council for Arts and Cultural Affairs (Cool Cities reviews). Michigan Creative Writers in the School Program – residencies in schools throughout Michigan, averaging at least one per year from 1986 to project close. Moderator, panel discussion, Art and Censorship. Editor and publisher of Sly Dog Press. Freelance computer consulting, desktop design and typesetting. Conducted seminars for the Genesee Intermediate School District for junior high school students at various times from 1980-1999. Tutored writing and research design. Twice guest editor of brix magazine, former contributing editor to New Pages, and editor of Artifact and Kwasind. Flint Cultural Center Programming Planning Committee. Organized and moderated "Couplets: Michigan Poets on Poetry," a monthly series filmed for public access television in winter/spring 1989. Helped co-ordinate reading series for Genesee Literary Center, Genesee Writers, the University of Michigan-Flint and others.

Featured poet: Schoolcraft College, Livonia, MI; Ann Arbor Poetry Festival, Ann Arbor, MI; Urban Institute for Contemporary Art, Grand Rapids; The Flint Public Library, Artsource Gallery, Buckham Gallery, Flint; Cranbrook Writers Conference, Wayne State University, Poetry Resource Center of Michigan, Macomb Community College, Detroit; Ann Arbor Poetry Slam, The Guild House, Ann Arbor; Centrum, Port Townsend, WA; Midwest Popular Culture Association Conference, Lansing, MI, and many others.

# SOUTH LYON EARLY CHILDHOOD CENTER PARENT ADVISORY BOARD BY-LAWS

## Article I: Name of Organization

The name of this self-governed organization shall be the South Lyon Early Childhood Center Parent Advisory Board (PAB).

## Article II: Location

The office for this organization will be located within the South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center (ECC) in South Lyon, Michigan. The services being provided by this organization will primarily be offered on the campuses of the South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center in South Lyon and any satellite school in the South Lyon School district.

## Article III: Purpose of the South Lyon Early Childhood Center Parent Advisory Board

The mission of the South Lyon Preschool Early Childhood Center Parent Advisory Board is as follows:

In support of the South Lyon Community Schools Preschool, Early Childhood Center and related programs, the Preschool Parent Advisory Board seeks to increase parental involvement and promote family activities. We gather and maintain a fund to support the programs, provide a forum where parents can share their ideas and concerns and be advocates for the programs in the community. We strongly believe that when parents become involved in the care and education of their children, everyone benefits.

## Article IV: Terms of Membership

General membership shall be open to any parent of a child currently enrolled in the South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center and/or related programs or staff.

## Article V: Officers of the South Lyon Early Childhood Center Parent Advisory Board

Officers of the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board are elected. Staff of the ECC and related programs may not act as officers.

Officer responsibilities of the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board are:

##  $ \underline{\text{President}} $

Schedule, set the agenda and conduct meeting. These meetings are open to all parents, and any parent may make a motion and participate in discussions.

Recommend sub-committees to the organization and monitor the function of subcommittees and related activities.

Act as liaison with the Director of the South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center.

Call special meetings of the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board.

Maintain bulletin board at ECC.

<a id="p44"></a>
*[Page 44 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=44)* (read by OCR; check the original)

##  $ \underline{\text{Vice-President}} $

Preside at meetings in the absence of the President, or at the request of the President.

Audit the checking account at least twice annually, preferably in December and May.

##  $ \underline{\text{Secretary}} $

Coordinate all communication between the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board and its membership.

Coordinate the recording and filing of minutes of the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board and its sub-committees.

Collect and review all correspondence of the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board.

##  $ \underline{\text{Treasurer}} $

Provide current financial reports at any regular meeting of the ECC Parent Advisory Board and at other times when requested.

Coordinate efforts to apply for grants, donations, reimbursements or any other funds for which the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board may be eligible.

Manage and account for funds collected, donated, and spent for the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board.

Initiate proposed annual budget and present at October meeting.

Provide a year in review financial analysis and report at the May meeting.

Act as signatory on all checks for the ECC Parent Advisory Board along with the South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center Director.

## South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center Director

Provide information and seek input on program and academic matters as well as provide information on budget and finances of the school.

Ensure establishment of ECC Parent Advisory Board.

Support and promote the Board's activities.

Post any materials distributed to members of the Board in a location that is accessible to all parents.

Act as signatory on all checks for the ECC Parent Advisory Board along with the Board Treasurer.

<a id="p45"></a>
*[Page 45 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=45)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Article VI: Elections

Elections shall be the first order of business during the September meeting. Elections shall be decided by a simple majority vote of those present at the meeting. New officers shall take office immediately following their election.

Nominations will be made at the September meeting or a prior meeting.

Any member is eligible to make nominations.

The South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center will be informed of the annual elections with the names, addresses and phone numbers of the new Board by October 1 of each year.

## Article VII: Term of Office

The term of office for an elected or appointed South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board member is one year, renewable by election or appointment, unless otherwise provided by the by-laws of the Board.

A vacancy in the membership of the South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board shall be filled by election or appointment in accordance with the by-laws of the Board.

## Article VIII: Meetings

General Meetings shall be held at least monthly, from August through May excluding December.

Notice of all general meeting dates and elections shall be published in the parent newsletter distributed by the Director of South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center.

The first meeting of each school year will be announced at the Parent Orientation Meetings held prior to the start of the school year. In addition, a calendar of meetings will be developed at the May meeting of each school year and distributed by the South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center Director to all parents children enrolled in the South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center Preschool and related programs.

Any change to the published meeting calendar shall require a simple majority of eligible voting members present at the preceding month(s) meeting(s).

Emergency meetings may be scheduled when necessary with 12 hours notification. Notification for either ‘regular’ or ‘emergency’ meetings may be in the form of facsimile, e-mail, telephone call or in-person contact.

Matters that require a vote to be taken of the voting membership include, but are not limited to the following items:

- A change in the By-laws.

A commitment of financial resources requires a simple majority of voting members present.

• Elections require a vote as described in Article VI, Elections.

<a id="p46"></a>
*[Page 46 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=46)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Minutes of all South Lyon ECC Parent Advisory Board meetings shall be recorded and retained for public record.

All the officers and representatives (or their alternates) are expected to attend all South Lyon Community Schools ECC Advisory Board meetings. The Advisory Board may consider replacing any of its members who have had three consecutive absences of regularly scheduled meetings. Before taking such an action, the Board's President shall ask the member in question if they wish to continue their service and convey their response to the full Board.

## Article IX: Committees

The duties of the chairperson of each committee are as follows:

1. Preside at all meetings of the committee.

2. Insure that all duties and responsibilities of the committee in his/her charge are properly and promptly carried out.

3. Appoint such sub-committees as may be necessary to fulfill the duties and responsibilities of the committee.

4. Communicate with the committee members to keep them fully informed.

5. Keeps the President and Secretary informed of committee actions and recommendations.

6. Forwards reports of all meetings to the President and Secretary.

The Committee Chairperson with the approval of the President shall choose members of all committees.

Committees will be created as needed.

## Article X: Dues

There shall be no dues.

## Article XI: Procedures

Roberts Rules of Order are the default procedures for this organization.

## Article XII: Budget

A revenue and expenditure budget will be prepared by the newly elected South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center Advisory Board Treasurer and presented for ratification at the October meeting.

A formal budget review can be called for by the Executive Board. Adjustments to the budget as a result of the review will require ratification at the next regular meeting.

Individual line item adjustments to budgeted expenditures, approved by the Executive Board can be made throughout the year without approval at the next regular meeting provided the increase does not exceed the lesser of $1,000.00 or 10% of the budgeted amount.

No Committee Member, Executive Board member or staff can spend over $50.00 without the approval of the committee chairperson, over $100.00 without the approval of the Executive Board, over $250.00 without the approval of the Executive Board.

<a id="p47"></a>
*[Page 47 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=47)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Article XIII: Dissolution

In the event that the program must discontinue its operation, the Advisory Board must dissolve. Reasons for dissolving the Board may include, but are not limited to, such examples as: there no longer being a need for the services the program offers; South Lyon Community Schools Early Childhood Center and the South Lyon Community Schools being unable to support the program's efforts; and/or proper funding for the program's operations can no longer be secured.

In the event of the dissolution of the organization, the Executive Board shall, after paying or making provision for the payment of all debts and liabilities of the organization, dispose of all the assets of the organization to the South Lyon Community Schools general fund.

## Article XIV: Amendments

Amendments to the By-Laws may be adopted at any meeting of the Board by a simple majority of the Parent Advisory Board members, provided that the amendment shall have been submitted in writing to the members not less than thirty days before the meeting, and that notice of the proposed amendment shall be included in the notice of the meeting. These amendments shall be presented to the Board at its next meeting.

## Article XV: Adoption

When signed and dated below by the Advisory Board's Secretary, these by-laws will be in full force. Such signature shall attest to an affirmative vote by not less than two-thirds of the members.

These By-Laws were approved at a meeting of the Board of Directors of the South Lyon Early Childhood Center Parent Advisory Board on March 14, 2007.

# CITY OF SOUTH LYON

This proclamation declares the first Friday in June to be National Gun Violence Awareness Day in South Lyon, Michigan to honor and remember all victims and survivors of gun violence and to declare that we, as a country, must do more to reduce gun violence.

WHEREAS, every day, over 100 Americans are killed by gun violence and on average, there are nearly 18,000-gun homicides every year; and

WHEREAS, Americans are 26 times more likely to die by gun homicide than people in other high-income countries, and

WHEREAS, protecting safety in the communities they serve is mayors' highest responsibility, and

WHEREAS, support for the Second Amendment rights of law-abiding citizens goes hand-in-hand with keeping guns away from people with dangerous histories; and

WHEREAS, mayors and law enforcement officers know their communities best, are the most familiar with local criminal activity and how to address it, and are best positioned to understand how to keep their citizens safe; and

WHEREAS, by wearing orange on June 7, 2024 Americans will raise awareness about gun violence and honor the lives of gun violence victims and survivors; and

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that I, Mayor Stephen B. Kennedy, of the City of South Lyon, declare the first Friday in June, June 7, 2024, to be National Gun Violence Awareness Day. I encourage all citizens to support their local communities' efforts to prevent the tragic effects of gun violence and to honor and value human lives.

Mayor Stephen B. Kennedy

# CITY OF SOUTH LYON ORDINANCE NO. ___

# COUNCIL MEETINGS

AN ORDINANCE TO AMEND THE CODE OF ORDINANCES FOR THE CITY OF SOUTH LYON, TO AMEND CHAPTER 2, ARTICLE II, SECTION 2-31 COUNCIL MEETINGS TO CHANGE CITY COUNCIL MEETING TIME.

## THE CITY OF SOUTH LYON ORDAINS:

### Section 1. Amendment to Chapter 2, Article II, Section 2-31, to read as follows:

### Sec. 2-31 Council meetings

The city council of the City of South Lyon shall hold regular meetings on the second and fourth Mondays of each month in the Municipal Building at 76:30 p.m. according to the official time currently in effect in the State of Michigan.

### Section 2. Savings Clause

Nothing in this Ordinance hereby adopted shall be construed to affect any just or legal right or remedy of any character nor shall any just or legal right or remedy of any character be lost, impaired or affected by this Ordinance.

### Section 3. Severability

The various parts, sections and clauses of this Ordinance are declared to be severable. If any part, sentence, paragraph, section or clause is adjudged unconstitutional or invalid by a court of competent jurisdiction, the remainder of the Ordinance shall not be affected.

### Section 4. Repealer

All other Ordinances or parts of Ordinances in conflict herewith are hereby repealed only to the extent necessary to give this Ordinance full force and effect

### Section 5. Effective Date; Publication

This Ordinance shall be published after adoption as provided by the Charter of the City of South Lyon, and shall take effect on July 1, 2024.

Stephen B. Kennedy, Mayor

# AGENDA NOTE Unfinished Business Item #2

MEETING DATE: May 13, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Recommendation from Planning Commission on November 16, 2023

AGENDA TOPIC: First reading of Ordinance to Amend the Official Zoning Map of the City of South Lyon by conditionally rezoning portions of the total 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel # 21-30-126-003, zoned I-1, Light Industrial, as follows: 24.39+/- acres of RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential and 1.63+/- acres R-3, Single-Family Residential. The remaining 8.14+/- acres will remain as I-1, Light Industrial.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: Elevate Land Holdings, LLC has petitioned the City and requested a Conditional Rezoning of a portion of a 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel #21-30-126-003, to allow 252 new multiple-family residential units, 3 single-family residential lots, and the remaining portion of the parcel to be developed with approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial development. The petitioner's request is pursuant to MCL 125.3405, which authorizes rezoning with conditions voluntarily offered by the owner of the property.

The City Council has previously considered the rezoning request and after much discussion about concerns of wetland impacts and traffic safety impacts, has seen alternative site plans regarding the location of the entrance drive off of Ten Mile. The original plan (dated November 8, 2023) shows a proposed re-location of the entrance drive off of Ten Mile. An alternative plan was presented in February 2024 with the entrance drive maintained in its existing location.

The City Council approved the first reading of the rezoning ordinance (associated with the site plan dated November 8, 2023) at the February 12th, 2024 meeting. The Council postponed action on the site plan (dated November 8, 2023) and requested environmental impact information of the re-located drive. The applicant provided a wetland impact study, along with the wetland delineation study and additional traffic information, at the March 11th Council meeting. The City Council made a motion to approve the second reading of the Ordinance, and the motion failed.

The applicant has since made some proposed modifications to the plan and submitted to the City a revised request for conditional rezoning and preliminary site plan approval. The revised request includes a modified design of the entrance drive to further minimize the impacts to the wetlands. This includes an arched bridge design which substantially reduces the amount of wetland fill required, in comparison to the previous design. In addition, the modifications include eliminating the boulevard to make the entrance drive and crossing as narrow as possible, shifting the entrance west as much as possible to minimize the

<a id="p53"></a>
*[Page 53 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=53)* (read by OCR; check the original)

crossing space, and eliminating the previously proposed boardwalk along Ten Mile that would have impacted the wetlands. The applicant is maintaining pedestrian circulation, but without an impact to the wetland. The applicant has provided an updated wetland impact assessment based on the revised design, which states that the proposed bridge crossing would involve less than one percent of the total wetland area.

The revised crossing design outlines the impact to the wetlands. It should be noted that the total area of wetlands that the bridge will cross is 2,700 square feet, but the bridge design reduces the amount of wetland fill to a total of 365 square feet, which accounts for the footings of the arch supports and a small corner at the northeast corner of the entrance.

The proposed ordinance contains the proposed Conditional Rezoning Agreement verbatim, but the Agreement will be presented separately for approval (Application, market analysis, and traffic study included only as exhibit, have not changed)

BOB LANGAN – BOBL@ELEVATE-PROPERTY.COM

RE: WESTLAKE CONDITIONAL REZONING - RECONSIDERATION

DATED: May 1, 2024

To Whom It May Concern,

Please accept this correspondence as a supplement to the enclosed plan revisions dated 4-25-24 as our narrative to further explain the modifications to the proposed Westlake Site Plan.

We are hopeful that the alterations to the plan merit the reconsideration of Preliminary Site Plan Approval which was recommended for approval twice at Planning Commission, and approved initially by the City Council.

It is our understand that, with the exception of the wetland impacts, the proposed use and configuration has garnered only positive comments from the two reviewing bodies and appears to be appealing to the City of South Lyon in all material respects.

Although we have dramatically reduced our overall wetland impact several times throughout the review process, a majority of Council based their recent vote (causing a motion to approve to fail) upon our proposal to impact the wetlands (although no formal statement to that effect was made when the motion failed). We have continued to stress the balance between traffic safety, which is included in our traffic consultant's report, the City's traffic consultant's report, the South Lyon Code of Ordinances, and EGLE itself who recognizes the importance in considering public safety in its evaluation of wetland permits.

The current plan goes far ‘above and beyond’ what EGLE would ever require, and we hope that Council views our efforts favorably and allows this project to move forward.

Our design modifications are as follows:

The crossing is as narrow as physically possible. We eliminated the boulevard and narrowed the entrance road while maintaining sufficient lane width and pathway width.

We moved the Lake Street entrance as far West as we safely could, and further swung the road as far West as we safely could, in order to have the shortest possible crossing.

<a id="p56"></a>
*[Page 56 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=56)* (read by OCR; check the original)

May 1, 2024

Most importantly and most dramatically, we are proposing an arched bridge, typically used in river or stream crossings, which vastly reduces the wetland fill. The only wetland fill required is a very small corner of the crossing, and the actual footings to hold up the arches.

To further reduce wetland impact, we re-routed the non-motorized pathway to eliminate the boardwalk along W. Lake Street. The non-motorized users (both residents and members of the public) will now be routed through the community on a scenic, non-wetland impacting route.

Our concern regarding traffic safety is embodied in multiple reports, including:

- Original Study by Fleis & Vanderbrink dated September 21, 2023

Fleis & Vanderbrink Access Management Review memo dated January 2, 2024

Fleis & Vanderbrink Driveway Location – Traffic Conflicts diagrams dated March 7, 2024

Transportation Improvement Association (TIA) memo dated February 8, 2024 (City consultant)

South Lyon City Code of Ordinances

In short, the traffic concerns are real, and unanimously supported by the multiple consultants who are qualified to make such evaluations. This is our motivation for continuing to work with the City on a project that minimizes wetland impact only to the extent necessary to maximize public safety.

We hope this supplement and the accompanying drawings are helpful in assisting City Council in striking an appropriate balance between traffic safety concerns and upholding the City Code of Ordinances (the duty of City Council) and wetland preservation concerns, which are regulated by the State of Michigan (and not the City of South Lyon).

We look forward to working with the City of South Lyon to bringing this exciting new project into reality. Please feel free to contact me any time with any further considerations or questions that may arise.

Northville, MI 48167

Regarding: Wetland Impacts associated with the proposed West Lake Place Residential Development located on the southeastern corner of 10 Mile Road and Dixboro Road, in the City of South Lyon.

### Dear Mr. Langan,

As a response to the City of South Lyon's request for a Wetland Impact Statement associated with the West Lake Place development, the following provides a summary of the proposed wetland impacts, and design considerations associated with the proposed project.

Wilson Road Group, Inc., (WRG) conducted a wetland delineation of the property in May of 2023 and prepared a Wetland Delineation Report for the property dated July 7, 2023. A total of three (3) wetlands (Wetlands A, B, and C) were identified and delineated during site activities. Due to either their size, or location, (within 500 feet from the nearest watercourse), Wetlands B and C would be considered regulated by EGLE under Part 303, Wetlands Protection, of the NREPA, 1994 PA 451, as amended.

##  $ \underline{\text{Wetland C:}} $

The proposed crossing impacts associated with the development are limited to Wetland C. Wetland C consists of a 9.54-acre, emergent wetland dominated by narrow-leaved cattail and reed canary grass, with wetland edge vegetation including sensitive fern, skunk cabbage and spotted touchme-not. These vegetative species have wetland indicator statuses of FACW and/or OBL indicating they are typically found in wetlands. WRG observed wetland hydrological indicators including saturation. The soils present within Wetland C appear to be Houghton and Adrian Muck which is identified on the NRCS national hydric soils list.

As shown on the attached 2023 aerial photograph, this wetland contains two distinct lobes of emergent wetland. A smaller western lobe, located west of the proposed crossing, and a larger eastern lobe, expanding east, southeast. Both lobes are connected by a narrow, much more linear portion of the overall wetland at the proposed crossing location. This narrowing characteristic is the result of the existing localized topography adjacent to the existing system.

<a id="p58"></a>
*[Page 58 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=58)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Please note, any proposed impacts involving the proposed wetland crossing would require a permit from EGLE prior to the start of any construction activities involving the regulated natural features located on the property.

## Proposed Bridge Crossing:

The proposed entrance and wetland crossing relate to a broader traffic safety concern involving existing roadway intersection/infrastructure and traffic patterns along 10 Mile Road. Traffic studies indicate having West Lake Development's entry/egress drive opposite Singh Boulevard is safer for motorists from a traffic management standpoint along 10 Mile Road than being located further west, closer to the Dixboro Road intersection.

If the City of South Lyon requires the entrance necessary, due to traffic safety issues, then the proposed wetland impact associated with its development is unavoidable. Although, consideration was given during engineering design activities to minimize proposed wetland impacts to the greatest extent possible as typically requested by EGLE. Also, it should be noted that EGLE is mandated to give proper consideration to “Public safety” concerns as part of their review of applications involving proposed wetland impacts. Numerous MDOT projects across the state every year involve this consideration.

Conveniently, the location of the proposed entrance and bridge crossing, opposite Singh Boulevard, is also the narrowest point of the on-site wetland system. Utilizing this existing characteristic of the natural feature allows for the least amount of proposed impact to the wetland due to being the shortest, most direct route. The proposed wetland impact associated with the wetland crossing will be approximately 0.06 acres or 2,700 square feet. This equates to an area measuring approximately 36 feet by 75 feet in size.

The proposed crossing consists of a triple arched bridge design which allows for the continued, unimpeded transition of water through the wetland system. The triple arch design also allows for the safe transition of wildlife as well as “daylighting” to occur at the water surface, beneath the proposed bridge structure.

It should also be noted that the smaller, western lobe of the wetland is the existing storm water discharge point of the existing storm water detention basin built as part of the West Lake Apartments Phase 1. Therefore, the western lobe would continue to receive treated storm water input from the basin, while the equalization culverts would also provide a transition of water further eastward, thus negating any potential of reduced hydrology to the broader system that would impacts its overall function and value.

## Wetland Impacts Resulting from the Proposed Driveway Crossing:

In summary, the proposed impacts to Wetland C associated with the development primarily involve the requested roadway crossing of the existing 9.54-acre wetland system located on the subject property. The bridge crossing proposes 0.06 acres of fill which represents less than one percent of the total overall size of Wetland C.

<a id="p59"></a>
*[Page 59 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=59)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Considering the proposed crossing, its location is at the narrowest point, between the two emergent wetland lobes, the design allows for the continued transition of water between the western and eastern portions of the wetland system as well as addressing/remediating traffic safety issues along Ten Mile Road, it is WRG's professional opinion that the proposed wetland crossing as described, will have a minimal effect on the wetland's functionality, habitat, and viability of the overall wetland complex.

Should you have any questions regarding this or any other matter, please feel free to contact our office at (810) 895-1219.

WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, 2008 PA 110, MCL 125.3405 et seq., as amended, certain conditions voluntarily offered by the Owner of the Property, may become a condition of rezoning the Property;

*[Page 71 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=71)* (read by OCR; check the original)

1.  $ \underline{\text{Conditional Rezoning}} $. Pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, MCL 125.3405, Owner agrees to limit the development and use of the Property as set forth in the rezoning conditions below as a condition of rezoning. Upon execution of this Agreement by the parties, the City shall effectuate the conditional rezoning of the Residential Property from I-1 District (Limited Industrial) to the RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), as legally described herein, on the City's official Zoning Map. This Agreement is valid and entered into on a voluntary basis and represents a permissible exercise of authority by the City pursuant to MCL 125.3405.

2.  $ \underline{\text{Rezoning Conditions}} $. Owner agrees to develop and use the Property in accordance with the following conditions which have been offered voluntarily in writing by the Owner as conditions to the rezoning:

a. Development and use of the Property shall be for a phased development of Multiple Family Residential and Single Family Residential on the Residential Property, and a phased development of Industrial buildings on the remaining portion of the Property zoned I-1 District (Light Industrial) in substantial conformance with the preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit B, and in accordance with the Time Limits described in Paragraph 6, below, consisting of:

(1) 24.40 $ \pm $ / - acres of Multiple Family Residential Property, 1.63 $ \pm $ / - acres of Single-Family Residential Property and 8.14 $ \pm $ / - acres of Light Industrial Property;

(2) 252 Multiple Family 1-, 2-, and 3-bedroom residential units on the Property rezoned RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential);

(3) 3 single family residential detached dwelling units on the Property rezoned R-3 District (Single Family Residential); and

(4) Approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial building space on the I-1 District (Light Industrial)

(collectively referred to as the "Project"). The Project will be developed in the phases as depicted on the preliminary site plan attached as Exhibit B and as follows: Phase 1 of the Industrial development, shall include one 19,840 +/- sq. ft. industrial building; Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 4-7, 84 units total; Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 8-12, 84 units total, and the clubhouse with ancillary amenities; Phase 3 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 13-18, 84 units total. The future development of the remaining I-1 (Light Industrial) zoned property and the R-3

<a id="p72"></a>
*[Page 72 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=72)* (read by OCR; check the original)

(Single Family Residential) zoned property will be developed in accordance with the underlying zoning district.

b. Development of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development to occur concurrently.

c. Constructed as part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development, the paving of 1,088 lineal feet of Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property.

d. At the time of Dixboro Road paving, the pedestrian pathway ultimately connecting to Huron Valley Trail, measuring 1,088 lineal feet along Dixboro Rd, will be installed from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property line;

e. As part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential Development, a payment of $36,200 to the City of South Lyon will be made for the future construction of the remaining 724 lineal feet from the southerly Property line and Huron Valley Trail.

f. As a part of Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development, a pedestrian pathway (connecting to the pedestrian pathway constructed in Phase 1) will be completed in the manner as conceptually depicted in the attached Exhibit B, exiting the Project at the Lake Street entrance, and connecting to the existing sidewalk that leads to downtown.

g. Provided that all necessary permits are secured by the applicable state, county, city or other governmental agency, the existing Lake Street driveway approach serving Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) will be removed and relocated during Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development in order to improve public and traffic safety. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

h. Owner shall secure and record all necessary shared access, use, utility, facilities, parking and other easements between the Project and adjacent development Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) as required by and in a form approved by the City.

Minimum floor area reduction to allow for 1-bedroom units to be a minimum of 600 sq. ft., 2-bedroom units to be a minimum of 768 sq. ft. and 3-bedroom units to be a minimum of 936 sq. ft. to meet market demand for 1 - 3 bedroom units and to allow preservation of nine (9) acres of wetlands and 50% of the existing woodlands.

572 parking spaces (which includes parking spaces within the existing Westlake Place Apartments, (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005), which will be available for use within the Project) provided for use by the Multiple Family Residential Property as recommended in the traffic study conducted by Fleis and Vandenbrink, which reduction will reduce impervious surfaces, increase available open space and area for community garden, recreation courts and a dog park.

<a id="p73"></a>
*[Page 73 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=73)* (read by OCR; check the original)

k. 223 parking spaces provided for the industrial use to be located on the portion of the Property within the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district. 67 parking spaces shall be provided for the Phase 1 Industrial development and the additional parking spaces are proposed because tenants and/or users have not been identified or secured for the Industrial use. Once a site plan is submitted for the specific configuration of the remaining Light Industrial district, the City shall determine the required number of parking spaces required or shall cause the Owner to conduct a parking and/or traffic study to establish a basis for the City to consider the appropriate number of parking spaces required. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

For the efficient use of the Property, considering the Project meets several master plan goals, the buffering requirement of the City's Zoning Ordinance between industrial and residential uses must be waived and, once tenants are secured and a site plan submitted, the City shall determine the appropriate buffer requirement considering the proposed industrial use.

3.  $ \underline{\text{Use of the Property}} $. Development and use of the Property shall be in substantial conformance with the approved preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit B, and shall be subject to and in conformance with the approval of the final site plan for the Property by the City Planning Commission. The parties agree that minor revisions to the approved final site plan which, in the sole discretion of the City Manager, do not significantly modify the proposed development or use of the Property or the site plan, may be considered and approved by the City administratively without the necessity of review and approval by the Planning Commission and/or without amendment of this Agreement and exhibits hereto.

4.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Laws}} $. Except as modified herein, development and use of the Property shall comply with all other applicable state, county and local requirements for land development and use, ordinances, codes, standards, regulations, requirements, and engineering design standards and requirements.

5.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Conditions}} $. Once commenced and established, the development and use of the Property must remain in compliance with the conditions of the conditional rezoning. Final Site Plan and as provided in this Agreement. Any failure to comply with a condition contained herein shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance and shall be

<a id="p74"></a>
*[Page 74 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/Council%20packet%20May%2013%202024.pdf?t=202405141323120#page=74)* (read by OCR; check the original)

punishable as provided for therein. Additionally, any such violation shall be deemed a nuisance per se and subject to abatement as provided for by law.

6.  $ \underline{\text{Time Limits}} $. The City and Owner agree that the following approvals and activities are conditions to this Agreement and if not completed within the following time limits, the Residential Property may revert back to the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning classification:

a. Final site plan approval within twelve (12) months from the date of this Agreement;

b. A building permit for the Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential and the Phase 1 Industrial development thereof, if applicable, is obtained within one (1) year from the date of final site plan approval;

c. A building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential.

d. A building permit for Phase 3 of the Multiple-Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential.

e. A final certificate of occupancy for the entire Multiple Family Residential, or Phase 2, or Phase 3 thereof if applicable, is obtained within thirty (30) months from the date the building permit was issued for said Phase.

7.  $ \underline{\text{Extension of Time Limits}} $. The City, in the City's exclusive discretion, may agree to extend the time limits in Paragraph 6 upon receipt of a written request from the Owner but the City shall not be required to grant such request for extension. Any extension granted shall be in writing signed by both parties.

8.  $ \underline{\text{City Right to Rezone}} $. In the event that the time limit conditions in Paragraph 6 are not met, nor extended upon mutual agreement of the parties pursuant to Paragraph 7; nothing in this Agreement shall be deemed to prohibit the City from rezoning all or any portion of the Property which is subject to this Agreement to another zoning district. Any rezoning shall be conducted in compliance with the City's Zoning Ordinance and the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act.

9. Owner Acknowledgments. Owner acknowledges that it voluntarily offered the conditions contained in this Agreement, including all Exhibits, in connection with the request for conditional rezoning of the Property and that such conditions were offered in writing. Owner acknowledges that upon the approval of the requested conditional rezoning and this Agreement becoming effective, the development and use of the Property shall conform thereafter with the terms and conditions of the conditional rezoning, this Agreement, and all the requirements regulating development and use within the zoning district as modified by the voluntarily offered conditions and with this Agreement and that any failure to fully comply with such conditions and/or this Agreement shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance, and shall be punishable accordingly. Owner acknowledges and agrees that the terms and conditions of this Agreement are necessary and roughly proportional to the burden imposed by the conditional rezoning of the Property, and are therefore necessary to ensure that public services and facilities will be capable of accommodating the development and use and the increased service of facility loads caused by the development and use, to protect the natural environment and conserve natural resources, to ensure compatibility with adjacent uses of land, and to promote the use of the Property in a socially and economically feasible manner, and to achieve other legitimate objectives authorized by law. Owner agrees that the terms and conditions herein do not constitute a taking of property for any purpose or a violation of any Constitutional right, and Owner agrees to be bound by each and every provision and conditions of this Agreement. Owner acknowledges and agrees that if the conditional rezoning becomes void for any reason, then no further development shall take place and no permits shall be issued unless and until a new zoning district classification for the Property has been established.

10.  $ \underline{\text{Entire Agreement}} $. This Agreement and Exhibits, together with any agreements referenced herein, constitutes the entire agreement between the parties with respect to the subject of this Agreement.

11.  $ \underline{\text{Binding Effect, Running with the Land}} $. This Agreement shall be binding on and inure to the benefit of the parties and their respective successors, heirs, assigns and transferees, and shall run with the land.

12.  $ \underline{\text{Amendments}} $. The terms of this Agreement may be amended, changed, or modified, but only by written agreement executed by the required parties. Any amendment of this Agreement shall be recorded in the Oakland County Register of Deeds.

13.  $ \underline{\text{Governing Law}} $. This Agreement shall be governed by, construed, and enforced in accordance with Michigan law.

14.  $ \underline{\text{Authority to Execute}} $. The parties each represent and state that the individuals signing this Agreement are fully authorized to execute this document and bind their respective parties to the terms and conditions contained herein.

15.  $ \underline{\text{Enforcement}} $. In the event of a proceeding to enforce this Agreement, a prevailing party may seek to recover its costs and attorney fees in addition to any other applicable and available relief.

16.  $ \underline{\text{Joint Drafting}} $. No provision of this Agreement shall be construed against or interpreted to the disadvantage of one party against any other party by any court or other governmental authority by reason of any determination or assertion that one party was chiefly or primarily responsible for having drafted this Agreement.

17.  $ \underline{\text{Conflicts}} $. In the event of conflict between the provisions of this Agreement and the provisions of another applicable ordinance, code, regulations, requirement, standard, or policy, the provisions of this Agreement shall prevail.
